# Naming Pass: Design (the client naming pass; the future item named by all 34 approved designs)

Date: 2026-11-24
Status: Draft, awaiting client approval, 2026-11-24. The six questions (Q1–Q6) were answered by the client on 2026-11-24, each with the recommended option A **(agreed; 0.2, 14.1)**; the document as a whole awaits the client's approval. Revised after the design check (consistency, clarity, tone and builder lenses), 2026-11-24: every change is a **(lead's call, after the check)**, listed in 14.4 with the items told plainly; no client answer is overridden.
Parents (immediate): every approved specification in `docs/superpowers/specs/` (34 documents) and `docs/superpowers/reviews/2026-11-23-cross-document-comparison.md` (reviews, "CC"). House style and template: `2026-11-24-charm-abilities-design.md` ("CA", segment 16, approved 2026-11-24, **the most recent template**). Abbreviations as in CA's front matter: MAIN, STAT, ABL, CON, ENM, ITEMS, W, AR, CN, DH, CL, TK, VG, SH, ARC, HR, CMP, RS (its change ids are RT-n), FL, RT (room and terrain; CA calls it "ROOM"), HZ, TL, EP, LE, IR, LP, EV, BR, XI, SV, CH, SK, HL, CA.

All 34 approved documents and the review were read for this pass through a harvest of **every player-facing name** (2,893 harvested entries, 2,769 distinct strings; 8.1), every naming-pass commitment and flag (205 mentions of "naming pass" across 27 files), and a cross-registry collision sweep (about 250 collision rows; 2.3). Every final name in 5 was grepped case-insensitively, as a whole word, across `docs/superpowers` (specs and reviews) while writing, 2026-11-24, and the **sites of every current name** in 5 were produced by the same script, not by hand (A.1, completeness critic). **This document edits no parent document** (13).

Items marked **(agreed)** with no parent document named are the client's answers of 2026-11-24 (final) to the six questions this design raised (Q1–Q6; 0.2 and 14.1), each answered with the lead's recommendation (option A). Parent documents' questions are always named with their document ("CH Q1", "HL Q1", "LE Q1", "LP Q5", "CA Q1"), so they never read as this document's.

**The lead's answers (0.1), the style guide (4), the register (5) and every mechanical rename** were accepted by the client by proceeding to the six questions. They are recorded here as the lead's answers, confirmed with this document when the client approves it, **including every told-plainly item (T1–T15) and every new lead's call listed in 14.2**, and the lead's calls and told-plainly items made after the design check (14.4).

**Scope (settled; the corpus).** The naming pass decides **player-facing names only**: display strings, labels, buttons, chips, notices, fallbacks, Plain-names labels and every `localeTermOverride`. Internal ids, field names and code identifiers are never renamed unless they reach the screen (rule N-Q, 4.13); the id-to-display map lives in one string table (defined in 9.1.1), so applying this pass changes data and text, not code. Where an id does reach the screen today (a greyed reason printed raw, a Grace id, a behaviour-profile name on a hover), this pass rules it **internal** and supplies a display string instead.

Markings follow CA:

| Marking | Meaning |
|---|---|
| **(settled)** | Restates a rule from an approved document |
| **(decided)** | A main-design client decision, restated |
| **(agreed)** | A client answer: Q1–Q6 of this document (2026-11-24, final; 0.2, 14.1), or one recorded in an approved document, named with its document ("CH Q1", "HL Q1"), restated |
| **(lead's call)** | Filled in by the design lead inside approved rules (the lead's answers of 0.1, the style guide of 4 and the register of 5 included); accepted by the client by proceeding to Q1–Q6; binding once the client approves this document |
| **(new lead's call)** | A lead's call made while writing, beyond the group proposals; listed in 14.2; confirmed with this document |
| **(lead's call, after the check)** | Made in answer to a design check (the synthesis critics of A.1, and the consistency, clarity, tone and builder check of 2026-11-24 after the client's answers, 14.4); never overrides a client answer |
| **(change NP-n)** | Amends an approved document; listed with its exact sections in 13. Rows that touch **settled**, **decided** or **agreed** text, or a client-confirmed name, are flagged ⚑ and name the answer that covers them (Q1–Q6, agreed, or the parent document's own answer) |
| **(told plainly)** | Narrows or re-reads approved text, contests the brief, an approved document or a critic, or states an unwelcome forecast; listed in 14.2 (T1–T15) and, after the check, 14.4 (TP1–TP7); confirmed with this document |
| **(request)** | Asks another owner to rule; changes nothing until that owner rules; the fallback is stated |
| **(hook)** | Owned by another design; this design states only what it needs |
| *(illustr.)* | Illustrative; never binding |

Rename types used in 5: **M** mechanical (a placeholder, an unclear name or a restatement becomes final; the doc owner applies it once this document is approved); **C** client (the current name is approved-final, client-agreed or client-confirmed, or the choice is a weighty taste call; the client decided it on 2026-11-24, and the row shows "(agreed, Qn)"; a mechanical, keep or retired row that only follows a question's answer carries the same mark); **keep**; **keep (waiver)** (kept with a recorded waiver, 9.3); **internal** (never printed); **retired** (registered so it cannot return; for a common word, only the renamed sense is retired, 9.1); **held** (kept on screen under a time-boxed waiver until a named gate, and never registered as retired; lead's call, after the check).

**The brief.** The naming pass is a separate client item that every approved design defers to ("Names are placeholders pending the naming pass"). Standing guidance: not an exact 5E port; the run is a dream and the premise stays uncommitted (RS 7.5); Grim Surreal tone; **combat is key**; contest the client or approved views where warranted.

**Change prefix.** This design uses **NP-n**. A grep for `\bNP-[0-9]+` across `docs/` (34 approved documents and the review) finds **0 hits** (re-run while writing, 2026-11-24).

**Validator.** **V24 `NamingPass`** is free: a grep for `\bV24\b` finds **0 hits** (re-run while writing). V1–V7 belong to RS, V8 FL, V9 RT, V10 HZ, V11 TL, V12 EP, V13 LE, V14 IR, V15 LP, V16 EV, V17 BR, V18 XI, V19 SV, V20 CH, V21 SK, V22 HL and V23 CA. V24 joins the consolidated suite only through **NP-1** (RS 11.2 suite table; SV 5.2 becomes V1–V24), as V23 did through CA-18. `V7.NameRegistry` stays the registry (RS 11.2, SV-20, settled); V24 is the rule set that runs on it (9).

---

## 0. At a glance

- **What the pass decides.** One final player-facing name for every placeholder in the corpus, one ruling for every flag the 34 documents sent here (2.2), and the standing rules (4) and machinery (9) that keep later content inside those rules. **2,893 harvested entries** resolve as: **329 renamed in whole or in part** (including client-decided ones), **2,428 final as written**, **83 final under a recorded waiver** (W-DREAM-1 included), **43 ruled internal only**, **7 retired or deleted**, **3 held** (8.2; Appendix B lists each; recounted by script after the check). The register in 5 has **337 rows**: 269 mechanical renames, 58 client renames, and the keeps, waivers, retirements and holds that needed a ruling.
- **Most names stand.** The world the client approved is specific and good. 84% of harvested names become final unchanged. Renames are spent where a rule is broken: SRD or another game's name, a real sacrament or slur, a premise leak, a name that lies (RC9), or a real collision a player would meet on the same screen.
- **The style guide in brief (4).** Two layers: a closed, plain **keyword layer** carries every rule; an open **flavour layer** never carries a rule a keyword does not also state on the same card. One register per category. Names may be whimsical but never lie. One word, one on-screen meaning. No D&D brand, no SRD name doing its SRD job, no exact match to another game. Premise words (dream, sleep, wake, nightmare, bed) only under one logged waiver list. No string is assembled at runtime. Upgrades keep their name. Every new name runs the Name Gate (4.10).
- **The biggest renames.**
  - *Legal and outside IP:* Fireball → **Detonation (fire)**, Scorching Rays → **Rays (fire)**, Blight → **Wilt**, Stoneskin Draught → **Slate Draught**, Holy Water → **Lamp Water**, Antitoxin → **Bitter Draught**, Second Wind → **Patch Up**, Surge → **Onslaught**, Sculpt → **Whittle**, Overwatch → **Ready Shot**, Volley → **Arrowfall**, Steady Aim → **Settled Aim**, Bull Rush → **Bowl Over**, Dreamfall → **Freefall**, Night-Mare → **Pale Mare**, Restricted Section → **The Chained Shelves**, Keepsakes → **Souvenirs**, the D&D 4e role set broken (Artillery → **Marksman**, Controller → **Hinderer**, Minion role → **Swarmer**).
  - *Sensitivity and premise:* Viaticum → **Parting Fare**, Last Rites → **Final Respects**, Totem → **Tipstaff**, Coffle → **Lashed Pair**, Lockjaw → **Jawbone**, Gout → **Deluge**, Lame → **Limping**, "Hang it?" → **"Shelve it?"**, Bedside Stand → **the Washstand**, Nightmare tiers → **Peril tiers**, Middle Dream → **Midwater**.
  - *Collisions a player meets:* Stairhead → **the Downstair** (Stairtop stays), Wary → **Watchful**, Rooted → **Tangled**, the printed word "catch" removed everywhere (TL family **Clasp**, Catch Breath → **Respite**, Caught → **Trapped**, Catcher → **Courier**, lane wording "Charms **hold** moments"), Keeper's Cart → **the Pedlar's Cart**, arena Device → **Counterpiece**, Cross-Reference (boss) → **Footnote**, Spur → **Urge**, Interject → **Speak Over**, Hold the Line → **Bar the Way**.
- **What the client decided (0.2, 14.1; agreed, 2026-11-24).** Six questions, each answered with the recommended option A: **Q1** the premise-word waiver list (W-DREAM-1); **Q2** three sensitivity renames inside agreed or approved text (Viaticum, Bedside Stand, Totem); **Q3** the Team Cherry cluster (Silk Archive → Cobweb Archive; Hollowfolk → the Fireside); **Q4** the difficulty-ladder name and MAIN's "boon" wording (Peril tiers; "offers"); **Q5** all three bundles of approved rules names renamed for SRD, IP or collision; **Q6** Vanguard, Shade, Arcanist, Herald and the Stairtop kept, Adept → Ascetic, Bastion → Rampart, no game title ruled. Every other row is mechanical.
- **What it adds.** V24 `NamingPass` (9.3), ten data files (srd names and allowlist, IP floor, echo list, sensitivity list, gazetteer, reserved premise words, retired names, waivers, glossary; 9.1), a string-table schema (9.1.1), a per-hit apply manifest that drives every owner's changes (13), a closed **Rules Glossary** of 83 core keywords at M2 plus the 32 conditions, the saves, the skill tiers and the rarity tiers (6.1; published as data), the **Plain names** setting extended from curses to charms, trinket lines, affixes and boss banners (6.4), and translator notes on every name (7).
- **Timing (10).** Mechanical finals apply wave by wave before any record of that wave is written (M1 names before M1 records, M2 names before M2 records), from the apply manifest; CMP-27 and SK-13 close now; the charm display word is ruled now, ahead of the M3 card-text freeze CA set; the Malady sensitivity read and the locale passes stay at M4.

### 0.1 The lead's answers in plain words (lead's call; accepted by the client by proceeding; confirmed with this document)

1. **What does the naming pass decide?** Every name a player can read. It does not touch code ids. For each of the 2,893 harvested names it gives one outcome (Appendix B), and for each flag the documents raised it gives one ruling (2.2). It also fixes the rules that later names must pass (4) and the check that enforces them (9), so the corpus does not drift again (RS risk 13).
2. **The style guide in one paragraph.** Rules travel in plain keywords (Bleeding, Warded, Resistant, Shove); flavour names (Hangnail, the Jailer of Hours, Nine Rivets) are labels that may hint but never lie. Each category has one register: classes are occupations, conditions are one plain adjective, items are an object plus one displaced adjective, bosses are titles, places are plain nouns made strange, curses and Curiosities may be whimsical because nobody reads them under combat pressure. Tone comes from voice (barks, Whence lines, Reveries), not from odd spellings. Dream, sleep and wake words stay off names except on the short waiver list W-DREAM-1 (agreed, Q1).
3. **The biggest renames, and why.** Twenty-odd names copy SRD spells or features doing the same job, or another game's title (Fireball, Second Wind, Overwatch, Dreamfall); four use real sacraments or a slavery term (Viaticum, Last Rites, Holy Water, Coffle); three are living illnesses or an ableist word (Lockjaw, Gout, Lame); one is a living people's sacred word (Totem). These are must-fix. Then the collisions a player would actually meet on one screen: Wary beside Weary, Rooted beside Routed, Stairhead beside Stairtop, a vendor called Keeper beside "keepers of the sleep", "catch" printed on cards that CA bans it from.
4. **What stays.** The four class names, the Stairtop, the theme names except the Silk Archive, the boss names, the Hollow and Last Hollow (camp scope only), the Book of Nights, Oddment and "Leave it by the fire", Reprieve Phial, Starting gear (final; SK-13 closes), the Wake theme and the Dreamer's Heart (under W-DREAM-1; agreed, Q1).
5. **What the client decided.** Renames of names the client agreed or that approved documents fixed as final, and the taste calls: premise words (Q1), the three sensitivity renames in agreed text (Q2), the Hollow/Silk cluster (Q3), the difficulty name (Q4), the bundles of approved rules names (Q5), and the class names and title (Q6). The client answered all six on 2026-11-24 with the recommended option A (0.2; agreed).
6. **What the pass will not do.** It does not rename code. It does not pick a game title (none is in the corpus; the store title needs a trademark clearance search, which is a legal step, 15 R11). It does not reopen agreed mechanics; where a name lied, it renames the name, except two places where the rule itself should change, which are requests to the owner (the Treadwheel stove, 5.5; Wayfarer's Belt keeps its effect).

### 0.3 Decisions in brief

| Topic | Decision | Where |
|---|---|---|
| Two layers | Closed keyword layer (83 core keywords at M2, published as data) carries every rule; flavour names are labels | 4.1, 6.1 |
| Registers | One register per category, with caps | 4.2 |
| Never lie (RC9) | Blind-guess test; keyword-in-flavour only if the thing applies that keyword | 4.4 |
| SRD | Function test (HOUSE-SRD-2), an explicit allowlist (conditions, rarities, six masteries, base families, actions, Extra Attack, Concentration, damage types) | 4.5 |
| Outside IP | Echo list; exact matches fail; clusters of one studio must be broken | 4.5 |
| Sensitivity | Blocklist outranks NR1; sacraments, slurs, living diagnoses, living sacred practices and hanging words (whole word or compound part) fail; gendered terms fail on HL registers and flag elsewhere | 4.6 |
| Premise words | Reserved; one waiver list W-DREAM-1 (agreed, Q1), checked on name and label fields by explicit word forms; The Waking City held under W-DREAM-HOLD-1 until M4 | 4.7 |
| One word, one meaning | Signature, Dormant, Plate, tick, kit, boon, Night, Catch each get one meaning | 4.8 |
| Internal-only families | Behaviour profiles, encounter templates, Stir parts, socket classes, EP catalogue titles, Grace ids, payoff kinds, Reverie label | 4.8 |
| Head-word cap | At most two live names per category share a head word (defined in 4.9) | 4.9 |
| Rule ids | Every id this document cites (N-A…, P-n, ECHO-n, CULT-n, HOUSE-SRD-n and the rest) is defined in one table | 4.13 |
| Retirement | Keyed by string, category and sense, so a kept sense of a common word stays legal | 9.1 |
| Applying the pass | A per-hit apply manifest (file, line, string, sense, action, NP row, final) drives every owner's changes; "every manifest line resolved" is the post-apply check | 13 |
| Name Gate | G1–G14 before any record | 4.10 |
| Validator | V24 `NamingPass` on `V7.NameRegistry` | 9 |
| Catch | Never a keyword or a name anywhere; ruled now for CA | 5.3, NP-14 |
| Stairtop / Stairhead | Stairtop stays; Stairhead → the Downstair (agreed, Q6) | 5.6 |
| Client questions | Q1–Q6 answered with option A (agreed, 2026-11-24) | 0.2, 14.1 |

## 1. Survey

**What the corpus already has.** Thirty documents carry the same front-matter line ("Names are placeholders until the client naming pass"), each with its own grep-checked new names and an "avoided because taken" table. That discipline worked inside each document and failed across them: each grep ran against the documents approved before it, so later documents collide with earlier ones in ways the earlier ones could not see (Stairtop and Stairhead; Keeper's Cart and "keepers of the sleep"; the Lullabist's Lullaby and the boss The Lullaby), and several fixes in one document re-broke another (the Catch family survived TL A.5 and was then banned by CA V23s). Classes 10 `NameCollision`, AR 17.1 rule 13, TK 15, V14 (f), V20s, V21 (g), V22 (e) and V23s each check a slice; none checks the SRD lists, other games, or the sensitivity lists as data (research brief, constraints G).

**What other games teach (research; the games named in the corpus's own references).**
- *Two layers.* Slay the Spire (a closed bold keyword set with tooltips), Into the Breach (function-named mechs, one-word statuses) and Hades (the slot noun: Strike, Flourish; flavour in the modifier) keep rules in plain words and flavour in names. This is the model for 4.1.
- *One register per category.* Darkest Dungeon (occupations for classes, "The X" bosses, plain afflictions), Outer Wilds (one naming source per culture: minerals for Hearthians, plants for Nomai). This is the model for 4.2 and the Fireside's trade-noun rule.
- *Hint truthfully.* Hollow Knight charms, Inscryption sigils. Where names only hint (Bloodborne runes, Sunless Sea stats), players need tooltips. This is RC9.
- *Upgrades keep their name.* Slay the Spire's "+" works; Hollow Knight's spell renames confuse.
- *Every one of them has a glossary* (Codex, Hunter's Journal, Ship Log, Pentiment's marked terms). This is 6.
- *Where they trip.* Real-world leaks (Slay the Spire's Lagavulin, Disco Elysium's Inland Empire), unpronounceable spellings (Yahar'gul), strings assembled in code (Caves of Qud). This is 4.6, N-G and 7.
- *Premise words.* Hunter's Dream, the Dream Nail and the Dreamers commit their games to a premise; Outer Wilds stays oblique for a long time. RS 7.5 wants the oblique route (4.7).

**What the law and the house rules allow (research, constraints; not legal advice).** SRD 5.1 and 5.2 are CC-BY-4.0 with attribution; CC-BY grants no trademark rights, so "D&D", WotC, setting brands and Product Identity names stay out (LEGAL-1, LEGAL-3). The house layer goes further than the law: RS 7.6 and HL-30 already ban SRD spell names and SRD and 5E class and subclass feature names on new display names (agreed RS Q3, HL Q1). The corpus also adopts SRD words on purpose (conditions, rarity tiers, six masteries, base weapon and armour families). A mechanical ban would fail the corpus's own vocabulary, so the pass needs a function test and an allowlist (4.5).

### 2.2 Every flag, ruled

Every flag in 2.1 has exactly one ruling. The compact index (full rows in 5; the harvest status of each harvested name in Appendix B):

- **Explicitly flagged to the pass (86 harvested entries; recounted after the check, 8.2).** Renamed in whole or part 46, retired 1, held 2 (the Sullens; Jactitation as a fallback), ruled internal 5, under a waiver 5, kept final 27 (Arch, Bossgate, Drape, Knit, Levy soldier, Oddment, "Leave it by the fire", Starting gear, Stairtop, Arrival, the Hollow, Book of Nights, Memento wall, the Newel, coat peg (prose only), the one-time version notice, and the boss-tied signatures, each with its reason in 5 or Appendix B).
- **"Catch" (CA F13, CA-12, TL A.5, AR 17.1 r13).** Ruled now: "catch" and "caught" are never a keyword or a name anywhere (4.8); the CA shape label stays an internal design label with no display word (the ◈ glyph is spoken "stored moment"); every printed occurrence is renamed (5.3). This closes CA 11's row "before M3 card-text freeze" ahead of time.
- **"Token" (TK 5.6).** Dropped as a charm display word; cards show the object noun; two item names take "Fob".
- **SK-13.** "kit" is the companion word only; the hero chip "Starting gear" is final; "Kit strip" → "Gear strip".
- **CMP-27.** "Levy soldier" approved; AR 12.3 already reads "Levy soldier" (AR l.1074), so CMP-27 closes with no AR change (NP-7, restated after the check).
- **CC-65.** "Sprawled" final; ENM 17.5, XI 5 and RS RC5 restate it; "Upended" retired (NP-6).
- **HR 19.** Spur → Urge; Interject → Speak Over.
- **VG 18.** Hold the Line → Bar the Way.
- **HZ "Cord".** Kept as the capitalised keyword; the binding-spell prose stops saying "cords" (NP-31).
- **BR "Prompts".** → "Boss-action warnings".
- **TL "Iced".** → "Frost-seized padlock".
- **HL spares.** The Frets retired; the Sullens held on the second-look list.
- **CH "the client confirmed these placeholders".** Kept unless a rule breaks; the two that change a confirmed concept (Hollowfolk; the Keeper's Cart's name, not its role) are Q3 (agreed: the Fireside) and a mechanical rename respectively (told plainly, 14.2 T10).
- **The CA "implies the Dreamer's age" check.** Run on every Heart rename (5.2); it rejected Hobby-Horse, Rocking Horse, The Toy Cupboard and Rattle (A.1).

### 2.4 Findings (told plainly)

- **F1.** The per-document greps were sound and the system still drifted: each grep saw only earlier documents, and nothing checked SRD lists, other games or sensitivity lists as data. The fix is machinery (9), not more care.
- **F2.** About twenty names are SRD spells or features doing the SRD job, another game's title, or a film or book phrase. The house rules (RS 7.6, HL-30; agreed) already forbid them; the documents' own checks missed them because the checks are internal greps.
- **F3.** Four names use real sacraments (Viaticum, Last Rites, Holy Water) or a slavery term (Coffle), the same ground on which HL rejected "Lent". One approved focus kind (Totem) is a living people's sacred word. These pass every existing validator.
- **F4.** The pass's own group drafts contradicted each other on about 40 names and proposed several replacements that broke the same rules (Mired is already a condition; Unbowed is already an armour property; Shelter is an armour property; Snap meets Snap Casting; Bile Slick breaks HL NR6; Running Noose and Picture-Hanger bring back hanging). The critics caught these (A.1); 5 carries one final per row.
- **F5.** The corpus does not ban SRD words; it adopts some on purpose. A pure blocklist fails the corpus's own core vocabulary (4.5 contests the literal reading of RS 7.6 and HL-30 NR2).
- **F6.** CA V23s's "never print catch on any surface" is right in effect but too broad as written (a substring ban on a common verb) and inconsistent with approved text that prints it ("Charms catch moments"; Shade's trigger Caught; TL's Lip Catch). 4.8 restates it as "never a keyword or a name"; ordinary prose in barks may say "catch".
- **F7.** HL-27's Plain names are unique only by prefix. Screen readers read the tag; "First Stumble" would be read the same for a Malady and a curse. Tags must be unique within scope (6.4).
- **F8.** Three approved names in agreed question text went to the client as questions (Viaticum in LE Q1; Bedside Stand's silhouette exception in LP Q5; "Charms catch moments" in CA Q1; agreed, Q2, Q5). **Corrected after the check:** they are not the only ones. Other renames also stand in parents' agreed or decided text: Lingerer (CH Q4, Q5; decided MAIN 4.7), "Seeded" on the hub menu and "a simple camp screen" (decided MAIN 4.7), Alms Crab, Offertory Plate and Prize Balloon (LP Q4), Mousetrap (EP Q1), Second Wind (VG Q1) and Hold the Line (VG Q3), and the Weaver's Cross-Reference (BR Q3's Gambit candidates; the Precentor's Crescendo there is kept). They change wording only, never the answer, so they stay mechanical rows: each NP row that touches them carries a ⚑ naming that parent answer, and the client's approval of this document covers them (lead's call, after the check; 14.4).

---

## 3. Approach

**How this document was made (lead's call).**
1. *Harvest.* One harvester per approved document listed every player-facing name with category, harvest status (approved-final, placeholder, unclear, flagged-to-naming-pass) and sites, and every naming-pass commitment and flag (2.1).
2. *Research.* Three briefs: naming conventions in comparable games, legal and cultural constraints, and the corpus's own naming rules (1).
3. *Collision sweep.* Four sweeps across the harvest (exact equality, near pairs by edit distance and shared first letters, external IP, tone and register).
4. *Style guide and group proposals.* A draft style guide and four group proposals (combat terms; items; world; interface and system).
5. *Critique.* Three critics (tone, clarity, completeness) read all five drafts.
6. *Synthesis (this document).* One final per row, chosen by the critics' lenses: the more truthful name wins; then the name with fewer collisions against the corpus **and the other winners**; then the shorter, more speakable one; then the one that keeps the client's world. Every winner was re-grepped (5, last column), and the current-name sites were generated by script (5, sixth column).

**Which side of a collision moves (lead's call).** The more important, more central or more approved name keeps the word: an agreed client name over a placeholder; a keyword over a flavour name; a boss over an enemy type ability; a class ability over a boss move when Classes 10 forbids equality (Cross-Reference stays with the Loremaster); the more truthful holder over the less truthful one (Routed over Rooted; Weary over Wary is the exception made for readability on plates, Q5).

**Candidates rejected in the synthesis.** Listed per row in 5's reason column and in A.1 (for example Landing, Exit Stair, Long Fall, Drop-through, Depth tiers, Regulars, Snap, Shelter, Shielding, Iron-Willed, Mired, Stuck, Bile Slick, Running Noose, Picture-Hanger, Rattle, Effigy, Psalter Crab, Benison, Treacle, Thump, Couch, Book of Runs, Shooter, Spieler).

---

## 4. The naming style guide (binding rules; lead's call; adopted by NP-2)

These rules are the **N-rules**. They add to, and never relax, RS 7.6, RC1–RC9, RS 8.0, Classes 10 `NameCollision`, W 7.4, TK 15, AR 17.1 rule 13, IR 4.6/6.1, V14 (f), HL NR1–NR10 and HL-27/30, SK 4.5.1–4.5.2, CH AN1–AN13, CA-12/V23s, SV 4.9 and XI-19 (all settled or agreed). Where a rule here narrows an approved rule, it says so and is listed in 14.2 or 14.4.

### 4.1 Two layers (N1)
- **N1a.** Every rule-bearing term (condition, damage type and family, ladder step, ability, save and skill, universal action and verb, resource and counter, area shape, awareness and morale state, refill tag, rarity tier) is a **keyword**: one plain word or a short fixed phrase from the closed Rules Glossary (6.1). No keyword has a synonym.
- **N1b.** Effect lines, previews, greyed reasons and logs refer to mechanics only through keywords and numbers.
- **N1c.** Flavour names (abilities, spells, items, notables, trinkets, charms, heirlooms, curses, enemies, bosses, places, hazards, traps, Curiosities, camp figures, given names, Oddments) are **labels**. The rule line sits above the name on every card (HL NR7 extended to all cards).
- **N1d (slot nouns).** Things that fill the same mechanical slot share a head noun and put flavour in the modifier (Hades pattern). Reserved slot nouns: **Signature** (the announced boss move only), **Attack** (the boss menu's basic move), **Device** (TL trap family only).

### 4.2 Registers by category (N2)

| Category | Register and form | Cap (English source, characters) |
|---|---|---|
| Conditions | One plain adjective or past participle; unique icon and colour | 12 |
| Damage types and families | Plain physical or elemental noun | 10 |
| Abilities, saves, skills, derived stats | Plain; one word or a fixed two-word phrase | 16 |
| Universal actions | Imperative verb first, 1–3 words | 16 |
| Weapon verbs | One imperative verb | 8 |
| Armour verbs | One adjective for the wearer's state | 10 |
| Properties | Truthful modifier or modifier + noun, 1–2 words | 16 |
| Classes | Occupational noun, one word | 12 |
| Subclasses | Role or craft noun, one word | 12 |
| Class abilities and pools | Verb-led or modifier + noun, 1–3 words; one sub-register per class (Vanguard drill commands; Shade thieves' cant; Arcanist workshop words; Herald speech acts) | 18 |
| Spells | Arcanist: template noun + "(family)"; Herald: a speech phrase | 16 |
| Enemy roles, ranks | Plain functional word; profiles internal | 10 |
| Enemy affixes | One truthful adjective; an exact condition word only if it grants that condition | 10 (a mirror affix that prints its condition's exact word takes the conditions cap, 12; lead's call, after the check) |
| Common enemies | Theme trade or role (+ material); Title Case on plates, lower case in prose | 18 |
| Bosses | "The <Role>", "The <Role> of the <Noun>", or "<Name>, the <Epithet>" | 28 (head 14) |
| Boss abilities | Diegetic verb phrase under the universal banner (RC7); never a party ability's word | 18 |
| Item base families | Plain object noun (SRD families allowed, 4.5) | 14 |
| Notables and signatures | Owner-trade, wear or material adjective + **family noun last** | 24 |
| Epithets | Suffix "of the <Noun>" only; never on a base that contains "of" | +14 |
| Bynames | "the <Agent-noun>", only after a Deed | 16 |
| Trinkets | <property-word>'s <kind> or <adjective> <kind>; the property is the verb (TK 15) | 22 |
| Charms | A small ordinary object; no kind word beyond the wave cap | 16 |
| Consumables | Function + container | 18 |
| Scrolls and wands | Follow the ability (ARC-20); wands never take epithets | 22 |
| Heirlooms | Wear or age adjective + family noun | 22 |
| Curse cost lines | Folk adjective (HL register 2) | 12 |
| Maladies | Obsolete humoral or theological noun (NR1) | 14 |
| Oaths | "Oath of the <Adjective> <Noun>" (NR5) | 30 |
| Themes | "The <Adjective> <Noun>" or "The <Noun> of the <Noun>"; one short form | 30 |
| Acts | One depth noun | 10 |
| Places | Plain topographic or architectural noun made strange by one modifier | 22 |
| Portal and terrain kinds | Plain rules noun | 10 |
| Hazards and Stirs | "<The Noun> <verbs>" or "The <Noun>" | 28 |
| Traps and locks | Object + mechanism noun | 18 |
| Curiosity cards | Whimsy budget: "The <object>" or a short imperative | 34 |
| Strands | Past participial phrase | 16 |
| Camp figures (the Fireside) | Period trade or craft noun, one compound; never a personal name or roster word (CH 5.1) | 16 |
| Companion kits | Occupational noun | 12 |
| Kit given names | Two plain words of material, wear or mark (SK 4.5.1) | 18 |
| Oddments | A resident trade's possessive + small object, no article (SK 4.5.2) | 24 |
| UI buttons / labels / notices | Sentence case; buttons are verb phrases of at most 4 words; labels at most 4 words; notices one sentence ≤ 18 words (relaxed after the check from 2 and 3 words, so that "To the Downstair", "Lower your head", "Not this time" and "Play a run code" pass; widths still checked at +35%) | 18 / 20 / — |
| Settings | Describe the effect | 24 |
| Meta options and difficulty | May use a comic or mismatched register; always truthful | 24 |

- **N2a (whimsy budget).** Long, odd or surreal names only where nobody reads under combat time pressure (Curiosities, Strands, Oddments, curses, camp figures, meta options, lore items, boss titles). The combat HUD reads at a glance.
- **N2b (one naming source per culture).** Recorded in the registry: the Fireside = period trade noun; Gaol = jail trades; Kennel = hunt trades; each theme's roster keeps its trade family (N-H).
- **N2c (caps are checked at +35%)** against the narrowest widget (7.1).

### 4.3 Form (N3)
- Title Case for names and keywords; sentence case for UI. Capitalisation never carries meaning (the "Cord"/"cords" lesson). No case transforms in code (L10N-5).
- Names never store an article; an `article` field and per-locale templates render it (L10N-4). A Curiosity whose article is part of the joke is a whole-phrase key with a translator note.
- Every countable name has an authored plural. Lists show "<n> × <Name>".
- Possessives only on notables, trinkets, heirlooms and Oddments, only with a trade or role noun, one per name, never a personal name, deity, saint, hero or Dreamer (NR3, AN4).
- At most one hyphen per name; no digits (digits on the map mean the room's clock); no apostrophes except the possessive; no diacritics; glyphs live in their own field with a spoken label (A11Y-2).
- **Templated numbers (lead's call, after the check).** A ladder tier or an upgrade rank is not part of the name: it is a numeric field rendered by a whole-string template, and the digit rule does not apply to it. Ladder tiers use Arabic numerals (`"Peril {n}"`, as the client's answer wrote it: Peril 1, Peril 2; agreed, Q4); upgrade ranks use Roman numerals (`"{name} {rank}"`: Whittle II, Urge I, Slot Renewal II). Each template is one key with a translator note (7.2).
- Coined words: at most three syllables, English sound patterns, one obvious pronunciation (read aloud).
- **Upgrades keep their name** (N-J): "Whittle II", "Urge I". Elites show by frame and affix, never by a prefix; only consumable strength tiers use "Greater".

### 4.4 Never lie (N4; RC9 applied)
- **N4a (blind-guess test).** Shown the name alone, a new player's guessed effect, element, target or threat must not contradict the rule line. An opaque guess passes when an icon and the rule line are present; a misleading one never passes.
- **N4b (keyword in flavour).** A flavour name may contain a keyword or keyword stem only if the thing applies, grants or reads that keyword. Passes: Staggering Shot (applies Staggered), Wardstone (grants Warded), Trapped (reads a unit in the Trapwright's trap). Fails (renamed in 5): Frozen Footman, Venom Oil, Unshaken, Grasping Roots, Chilled Pour, Iron-Willed, Hidden Reserves, Shielding.
- **N4c.** Element words imply that element (no "thunder", "shock" or "fire" on non-matching effects); "Pack" means the inventory place; "bar" means a Grille; "trap" means a TL trap.

### 4.5 SRD, legal and outside IP (N5; contests the literal reading of RS 7.6 / HL-30 NR2, told plainly T1)
- **N5a (hard floor).** No WotC brand, Product Identity monster, named-wizard prefix, setting or character name (LEGAL-1, LEGAL-3). No "compatible with D&D" claim.
- **N5b (multi-word SRD names)** fail as exact phrases, case-insensitive (HOUSE-SRD-1).
- **N5c (single-word SRD names)** fail only where the thing does broadly the SRD job (HOUSE-SRD-2): a "Haste" that grants actions fails; "haste" in a bark passes.
- **N5d (the allowlist, `srd_allow.json`).** Adopted with attribution and passing: condition names; rarity tiers; the six adopted 5.2 masteries (Cleave, Graze, Nick, Push, Topple, Vex); SRD weapon, armour and gear base families; the six abilities and saves; the ten damage types and three families (their theming stays open, STAT 8.1, agreed); the universal actions Dash, Disengage, Dodge, Help, Hide, Search, Shove, Grapple, Attack and Opportunity attack; Extra Attack; Concentration; short rest; spell slot; Pack Tactics and the cited traits; Keen (sense trait). Any other SRD hit fails.
- **N5e (echo list, ECHO-1..4).** An exact match to another game's named item, ability, area, currency or title fails when the name is a coined or compound word, or when it names a class, subclass, theme, hub or any store-facing thing (N5g); an ordinary dictionary word used as an ordinary word elsewhere (an affix, a condition, a keyword) is flagged and read (refined after the check, so that Tangled, Undying, Onslaught or Reflection do not fail on a film or game of the same word). A shared generic word in a matching mechanic is flagged. Two or more echoes of one studio in one scope must be broken by a rename (cluster test). Seed list: Darkest Dungeon, Hollow Knight and Silksong, Hades and Hades II, Souls and Bloodborne, Slay the Spire, Mass Effect, Dreamfall, plus the game titles found (Overwatch, Bastion, Condemned, and after the check Windblown and Rampart, W-28).
- **N5f (real world, N-L; new lead's call, told plainly T2).** A gazetteer check against real places, brands, products, people and well-known film and book titles fails exact matches, under the same dictionary-word rule as N5e. This is new, not text in RS 7.6 (research, corpus-rules: the brief overstated RS 7.6); real people and places already fail through HL NR3.
- **N5g (store-facing names).** The title and any name used in marketing (hub, classes, themes if marketed) get a trademark clearance and exact-match search before a store page (SEARCH-1); that is legal's step.

### 4.6 Sensitivity (N6)
- The blocklist (`sensitivity.json`) **outranks NR1** (MH-1; told plainly T3): an obsolete clinical term that is now a slur fails however old it is.
- Fails: living diagnoses and stigma words (MH-2), slurs and slur-verbs (CULT-2), a living people's sacred practice (CULT-1), real religions' figures, sacraments and holy-war words (CULT-3; RS 7.6's "invented liturgies only"), hate-symbol names and numbers (CULT-4), and suicide and self-harm words (new lead's call; the "Strung Marionette" precedent).
- **Hanging words (restated after the check).** The list is explicit word forms, matched as a whole word or as a part of a compound (hyphenated or closed), never as a bare substring: **hang, hangs, hanged, hanging, hangman, hanger, noose, gallows, gibbet**. So "change" and "exchange" pass; the matcher is the whole-word and compound-part matcher of G9 (4.7), and the 4.10 substring filter does not apply to the sensitivity list. The pass's own strings that met it are renamed: "Hang it?" → "Shelve it?" (5.2.12), the Wake skin "drawn curtain" → "drawn arras" (5.6.19), the lock state "hanging" → "dangling" (5.2.37), the Heart skin "bed hanging" → "canopy valance" (5.2.39); Gallows Penny stays a rejected CA candidate. **Hangnail** (CA's base charm and this document's example name) is a compound part match kept under waiver **W-25**: a body word (a torn sliver of nail) with no hanging sense.
- **Gendered terms (scoped after the check).** HL NR2 bans gendered terms on heirloom and curse strings; that scope is kept as a **fail** (HL heirloom, curse, Malady and Oath registers). Everywhere else a gendered term is a G8 **flag**, read by the tone review: a period role title on a boss or enemy (The Night Governess, Madame Ashes, Still Footman, The Strongest Man Who Ever Lived) and the agreed role word Marksman (Q5) are kept under waiver **W-26**. A new gendered name outside that waiver needs its own recorded waiver.
- Church titles (Deacon, Precentor) are generic usage and count under RS 7.6's church cap; the sensitivity read sees them.
- **'bile'** never appears in a display string except Atrabile (HL NR6, settled). The Gut theme says "gall" throughout: Gall Slick, gall square, The Gall Low (5.1.27, 5.2.35, 5.2.36; the terrain renames made after the check), and Bile Spit → Sour Spit.

### 4.7 Premise words (N7; RS 7.5, AN4; agreed, Q1)
- **Reserved:** dream, dreamer, sleep, slumber, asleep, wake, waking, woke, awake, nightmare, reverie, lucid, lullaby, bed, mattress, pillow, cradle, crib, and the CA age-implying list (swaddle, milk-tooth and the like).
- **Names may not contain them** except on the waiver list **W-DREAM-1** (agreed, Q1, option A):
  - **(a) UI furniture that names the run's frame:** Dream card, Dream track (and its dream-track ribbon), Dream step, Dream's Due (and its tags, "The dream's due: for <name>"), Dream map, "What the dream kept", "New in the dream", "the dream unravels", Dream Rules pages, the Unwoken Roll, "You did not wake", Asleep and Dormant (approved conditions and states). **Added after the check (lead's calls, 14.4):** Dreambook pages (XI, Later) and "dreamed option(s)" (LE), the same frame.
  - **(b) Named F5 Heart and Wake-theme content, listed one by one:** The Endless Wake; The Dreamer's Heart; Sleepwalker; Insomniac; Lullabist; The Lullaby (boss); Lullaby Glyph; Nearer Waking; Fitful Sleep; Foot of the Bed; the Turned-Down Bed; The Dark Under the Bed; Hand Under the Bed; The Bed Turns Over. **Added after the check, one by one (lead's calls, R9; 14.4):** The Wake for You (EV, Wake theme); The Bedpost Ladder (EV, Heart); the Heart places "bed clearing" and "pillow rooms" (FL); the Heart Keg skin "dust pillow" (RT) and the Heart vent "the dust-pillow seam" (HZ). "The Dreamer" never stands alone as a name; it appears only inside The Dreamer's Heart and in lines.
  - **(c) Everything else is banned**, including "bed", "pillow" and "mattress" on any name not listed in (b). The Heart has no blanket licence: (b) is a list, not a scope (R9). Renamed for (c) after the check: Dream Fragments → Fragments, the Heart skins "bed hanging" → "canopy valance" and "crib bars over glass" → "rail bars over glass" (5.2.34, 5.2.39, 5.2.40); the "Heart sleeping-draught nurse" example is deleted (5.2.38).
- **The Waking City is held, not retired** (agreed, Q1): it stays on screen under the time-boxed waiver **W-DREAM-HOLD-1** until the M4 content lock, and is then renamed or waived by the client. It is kept out of `retired_names.json` so that the client's waiver path stays open (4.11).
- **How G9 and V24 r7 match (lead's call, after the check).** `reserved_premise.json` holds explicit word forms (dream, dreams, dreamed, dreamer, dreamt, dreamy; sleep, sleeps, sleeping, sleeper, asleep, sleepwalker; slumber; wake, wakes, waking, woke, woken, awake, unwoken; nightmare; reverie; lucid; lullaby; bed, beds, bedside, bedpost, bedtime, bedsheet, bedroom, bedding, bedclothes; mattress; pillow; cradle; crib; swaddle; milk-tooth). A form matches as a whole word or as a part of a compound (Bedside, dust-pillow), never as a bare substring, so Barbed, Nabbed and Embed pass. Recorded exceptions: **bedroll(s)** (camp gear, the client's own word in CH; no Dreamer's bed implied) and "wake" in the sense of a ship's wake (none in the corpus yet). **r7 checks name and label fields only** (the string table's `field` type, 9.1.1): a line or notice (a sentence) follows NR8, AN4 and the tone review instead (T4), so "The dream owes you the rest." and "The Sanctum will not let you sleep." are lines, not names.
- Flavour lines may use premise words within NR8 and AN4; names may not (C9 of the style draft; told plainly T4).
- **Never**: any name implying the Dreamer's identity, face, **age** or bed outside list (b); any waking promise; any hero motive.

### 4.8 One word, one meaning (N8)

| Word | Its one on-screen meaning | Every other use |
|---|---|---|
| Signature | The boss's announced move | Weapon verb signatures show the property name; subclass "Signature Technique / Tricks option" show as their own names; "Signature item" shows as "the boss's prize" (agreed, Q5); `signature cache` internal |
| Dormant | The enemy awareness state | Item line "? untold" (agreed, Q5); charm pip "Idle" |
| Plate | The unit information plate | Armour shows "Plate armour"; the trap trigger is a "pressure slab"; Offertory Dish |
| Hide | The universal action | Armour shows "Hide armour" |
| tick | The time unit | Shield glyphs spoken "shield mark / broken mark"; the Dream tick cue has no text |
| kit | A companion package | Hero gear "Starting gear"; "Gear strip"; Herald picks "Shield hand / Focus hand" |
| boon | The starting boon | MAIN 4.5 pool says "offers" (agreed, Q4) |
| Night | Book of Nights and the Night Usher, Night Governess (proper names) | No unit of play, tier, market, card or Wager room |
| Catch / caught | Never a keyword or a name | Clasp, Respite, Trapped, Unbraced, Courier, Lip Clamp, grab square, Forecourt Top-up, Hunch badge, "Facing the door", "Charms hold moments"; ordinary prose in barks may say "catch" |
| Strike | The attack-roll word in rules text | Boss menu "Attack" (agreed, Q5); Stir part internal |
| Hollow | The camp phase and Last Hollow (camp scope) | Hollowfolk → the Fireside (agreed, Q3); "the next camp" |
| Stair | The Stairtop (hub) and the Downstair (floor exit) | Hub station the Banister |
| Run | The unit of play (a run, the run code, the run record) | The Commanded word "Run" (agreed, Q5) prints only after "Commanded:" with the keyword icon; recorded under W-27 (after the check) |
| low | The HP plate mark "low" (DH-20) | The DC tier reads Mild (5.3.25, after the check) |
| Ward | Warded and items that grant it | Role tag Buffer; -ward neck suffix kept as a recorded waiver (W-13) |

**Families ruled internal (never printed; V24 r6).** Behaviour profiles (Charger, Holder, Kiter, Mob, Automaton, Boss, Raider, Protector, Disruptor, Commander, Stalker, Ambusher, Opportunist, Sentinel); encounter templates (ENM 17.4; the Lore preview shows EP formation names); HZ Stir parts (Strike, Turn, Portal, Act, Taint, Emit, Overturn, Mute round); BR Counterpiece payoff kinds (Cancel, Deny, Divide, Divert, Sate, Expose, Release); socket classes; EP catalogue titles (G1…H6) and FT1–FT3; EV Grace ids (G-Ward…; the chip shows "Grace: Warded"); FL Site and SiteRule; CA shape labels (Spend, Catch, Keep, Shaper); HL Bequest; IR Reverie label (the line shows in the Whence pane unlabelled); the Antechamber (the room is the Forecourt on screen); reaction-policy and greyed-reason ids (each gets a display key: the reaction policies in 5.9.14; every greyed-reason id through its document's list in the apply manifest, before M1 records, 9.1.1); CMP Gap card, Free cards, slot ladder; SV build tag, check character and preset; `MimicPlacement` ("mimic" never on screen).

### 4.9 Head-word cap (N9)
In one category (and across the class layer), at most **two** live names share a head word or stem of four or more letters. **Head word (defined after the check):** the last noun of the name (Fast **Grip**, Wire **Loop**, Toll-Taker's **Shield**); for a verb phrase, its first word (**Hold** the Line, **Bar** the Way); for "X of the Y", X; a compound counts each part (Hound-Collar counts Collar). V24 r8 computes it from the record's `register` row. Over the cap → rename (the CA Knot rule made general). Currently at cap after this pass and watched: Knot, Stone, Bone (Twitch Bone, Wishbone; Jawbone ships in a different wave, W-16), Pebble, Bead, Long, Far, Last, Deep, Hold, Second, Sure, Ring, Iron, Hand, Final, Hour, Grip (Short Grip, Fast Grip; Clench became Vicelike after the check). "-ward" on the neck lane and "Ember" items are recorded cluster waivers (W-13, W-1).

### 4.10 The Name Gate (N10; how a new name is checked)
Every new or renamed player-facing name passes, in order: **G1** register (4.2); **G2** blind guess (4.4); **G3** keyword collision (exact, keyword stem inside flavour, keyword pairs at edit distance ≤ 2, shared first three letters within a family, homophones; the AN5 tokens Back and Down count as keyword stems for place and action names, added after the check; a negation pair inside one family, such as Found and Unfound, is exempt from the edit-distance test because the two differ by icon and colour); **G4** flavour collision (exact equality across all registries fails; shared head word in a category, edit distance ≤ 2, same first four letters and length band, and filtered substrings flag); **G5** SRD (4.5); **G6** IP floor; **G7** echo; **G8** sensitivity; **G9** premise; **G10** real world; **G11** localisation (translator note, +35% pseudo-loc fit, no stitched fragments, locale blocklist); **G12** accessibility (no TTS homograph, not a UI button verb, SSML hint for obscure words, no colour-only sibling pair); **G13** tone by depth (RS 7.4); **G14** search and trademark (store-facing names only). A flag needs a recorded waiver (reason, approver, date); a fail blocks the record. G3–G10 run in V24 (9.3: G3 in r3, G4 in r1 and r3b, G5, G6, G8 and G10 in r4, G7 in r5, G9 in r7); G1, G2, G11–G14 are review gates with recorded results.

**Substring rule (contests V14 (f), NR6 and V20s as written; told plainly T5).** Substring checks run only where the shorter string is a keyword, or is five or more letters and both names sit in the same category or on the same surface. Everything else uses whole word, head word and edit distance. Unfiltered substring greps on "Ward" (223 hits), "Answer" (82) or "Fire" drown the real hits. Recorded waivers stay valid.

### 4.11 Lifecycle (N11)
`draft → placeholder (frozen, grep-checked) → proposed (naming pass) → approved-final → shipped`; a rename of an approved-final name needs the client, an NP or later change id, owner restatements in every citing document, and a post-apply check that every apply-manifest line for the old name is resolved (13). Old names go to `retired_names.json` and never return with a new meaning. **Retirement is scoped (after the check):** a record retires a string in one category and sense (Hidden as a trap grade, not the condition; Dormant as an item line, not the enemy state), and lists the senses that stay live. A **held** name is not retired (The Waking City, W-DREAM-HOLD-1).

### 4.12 Smaller rules
- **N12 (epithets).** A base name never contains "of"; wands never take epithets (fixes "Sigil of Aid of ?").
- **N13 (Arcanist overrides).** A `variantOverrides` name must pass G5; if it fails, the "Template (family)" form wins and the scroll is renamed.
- **N14 (example names).** Example text uses only registered coined names (Brask, Tolly); never SRD monsters or real surnames.

### 4.13 Rule ids used in this document (lead's call, after the check)
The ids below come from the pass's three research briefs (naming conventions, legal and cultural constraints, the corpus's own rules); they are defined here so that no ruling rests on an unpublished brief. Each points at the N-rule that carries it.

| Id | Rule in one line | Carried by |
|---|---|---|
| N-A | Previews and effect lines print the keyword, never a synonym | N1b |
| N-B | A rider prints the exact condition keyword it applies (Bleeding, not "Bleed") | N1a, N1b |
| N-C | One slot noun per mechanical slot (Signature, Attack, Device) | N1d |
| N-G | Tone comes from voice, not odd spellings; coined words are speakable | N3 |
| N-H | One naming source per culture or theme | N2b |
| N-I | Premise words are reserved; never a rules keyword | N7 (4.7) |
| N-J | Upgrades keep their name; ranks are templated | N3 (4.3) |
| N-K | A keyword or its stem in a flavour name only if the thing applies it | N4b |
| N-L | Real-world gazetteer check | N5f |
| N-M | Lore never redefines a keyword | 6.1 |
| N-Q | Internal ids are never renamed unless they reach the screen; then they get a display key | Scope, 4.8, 9.1.1 |
| P-1 | Errors and system notices are plain words, never premise or flavour words | 4.2 (UI row), 4.7 |
| P-2 | One word, one on-screen meaning | N8 (4.8) |
| P-3 | Raw ids never show on screen | 4.8, V24 r6 |
| ECHO-1 | The seed list of studios and their category names (Hades' Keepsakes, Darkest Dungeon's Heirlooms) | N5e |
| ECHO-2 | An exact match to another work's name (game, film, book) | N5e, N5f |
| ECHO-3 | The cluster test: two echoes of one studio in one scope | N5e |
| ECHO-4 | A partial echo of a title's distinctive stem (Silk-, Dream-) inside a cluster | N5e |
| MH-1 | The sensitivity blocklist outranks NR1's "obsolete for a century" | N6 (4.6) |
| MH-2 | Living diagnoses and stigma words fail | N6 |
| CULT-1 | A living people's sacred practice fails | N6 |
| CULT-2 | Slurs, slur-verbs and slavery terms fail | N6 |
| CULT-3 | Real religions' figures, sacraments and holy-war words fail | N6 |
| CULT-4 | Hate-symbol names and numbers fail | N6 |
| HOUSE-SRD-1 | A multi-word SRD name fails as an exact phrase | N5b |
| HOUSE-SRD-2 | A single-word SRD name fails only when it does the SRD job (function test) | N5c |
| HOUSE-SRD-4 | SRD and 5E class, subclass and class-feature names and their echoes fail (HL-30) | N5b, N5c |
| LEGAL-1 | SRD 5.1 and 5.2 are CC-BY-4.0 with attribution and grant no trademark | N5a |
| LEGAL-2 | `srd_names.json` is built from the SRD documents, never from memory | 9.1 |
| LEGAL-3 | No WotC brand, Product Identity name or compatibility claim | N5a |
| L10N-1 | +35% for names, +50% under 10 characters | N2c, 7.1 |
| L10N-3 | Whole-string keys with named placeholders | 7.2 |
| L10N-4 | Articles and possessives live in fields, not in names | N3, 7.2 |
| L10N-5 | No case transforms in code | N3, 7.1 |
| A11Y-1 | No colour-only sibling pair | 7.4 |
| A11Y-2 | Every glyph has a spoken label | N3, 7.4 |
| A11Y-3 | Pronunciation hints; obscure names announce the Plain label first | 6.4, 7.4 |
| A11Y-4 | No name is a UI button verb or a TTS homograph | 7.4, G12 |
| CONF-1 | Confusable near pairs (first letters, length band, edit distance) | G4 |
| CONF-2 | A stem used in more forms than the cap allows | N9 |
| SEARCH-1 | Trademark and exact-match store search before a store page | N5g, G14 |
| PROC-2 | Each shipping locale runs its own blocklist, echo list, collision grep and native read before M4 lock | 7.3 |

RS's RC1–RC9, CH's AN1–AN13 and HL's NR1–NR10 keep their own documents' definitions.

## 5. The name register (lead's call; C rows agreed, Q1–Q6; applied by NP-4 to NP-38)

One row per ruling. **Final names are binding once the client approves this document** (C rows as the client answered them on 2026-11-24; each shows "(agreed, Qn)" in its type column, as does every mechanical, keep or retired row that only follows a question's answer). Names not in this register and not in 4.8's internal families are **final as written** (Appendix B lists every harvested name with its outcome). Columns: the current name; the final; the reason (the rule it breaks, and the candidate rejected where a critic rejected one; "(after the check)" marks a final changed in answer to the design check, 14.4); the **sections where the current name stands**, regenerated after the check by the same script as the sites (for a common word, the hand-cited sections of the renamed sense, each checked by script to contain the word); the **sites of the current name**, generated by a whole-word, case-insensitive script over the 34 specs and the review (document abbreviation and hit count; history notes are included, so restatements can be checked against this list); the type (M mechanical, **C** client ⚑ with the agreed question that decided it (0.2, 14.1), keep, waiver, retired, held); and the whole-word hit count of the final name across the same corpus (0 is free; any hit was read, A.2).

Where a current name is a common word with several senses (Open, Hidden, Strike, Set, Spare, Charge, Hold, Kick), the row renames only the sense in its first column; the other senses stay, and its retirement is scoped to that sense (4.11, 9.1). Its hit count covers every sense; which hits change is settled line by line in the apply manifest (13), not by the count. Internal ids named in a row stay unchanged (N-Q).

## 6. Glossary and Plain names (lead's call; NP-2)

### 6.1 The Rules Glossary (closed)
One entry per keyword: name, one-line definition, icon, colour, tooltip, related keywords, translator note. Reachable from every keyword tooltip in combat and out. **Closed**: adding a keyword is a lead's call and every document that uses it is updated. Lore text never redefines a keyword (N-M).

**Tier 1, the HUD tier (M2): 83 core entries listed below, plus the 32 conditions, the saves, the skill tiers and the rarity tiers their source documents enumerate** (corrected after the check from "60"). The exact list ships as data, `glossary.json` (9.1), one id per keyword; V24 r1 and r3 read keywords by that id.
- **Conditions:** the M1+M2 set of CON 16 (32 of 32; settled), each with its icon. Later conditions enter as their content ships, after the renames in 5.3 (Tangled, Frenzied, Heedless, Limping as flavour of Scarred).
- **Damage:** the ten damage types and three families (STAT 8.1; on the SRD allowlist; theming open); the ladder **Weak, Normal, Resistant, Immune** (one meaning each; "Resisting (type)" prints "Resistant (type)", Q5).
- **Abilities, saves and skills:** the six abilities with Presence (STAT 5.1), the saves (Will printed as "Will", never inside a boss move), the skill tiers including **Mastery** (skills only; never on a verb, W 7.4).
- **Universal actions:** Attack, Move, Dash, Disengage, Dodge, Help, Hide, Search, Shove, Grapple, Slip past, Probe, Steal, Treat, Tend, Escape, Rally, Frighten, Opportunity attack, Trigger Shot, Hear out (camp), Tap the wall.
- **Commanded words (CON 18.3; agreed, Q5; added after the check):** Come, Run, Crouch, Wait, each printed after "Commanded:" (W-27 for Run).
- **Resources and counters:** HP, Recovery, Supply, Coin, spell slot, pool, Concentration, Dream's Due (W-DREAM-1 a).
- **Area shapes:** Burst, Aura, Cone, Line (printed as icons with a tooltip).
- **Awareness and morale:** Dormant, Stationary, Patrolling, Alerted, Engaged, Pursuing, Searching, **Watchful** (agreed, Q5); Emboldened, Demoralised, **Routed**, Cowed.
- **Rarity:** the SRD 5.1 tier names (ITEMS 12.5; attributed).
- **Exploration grades:** trap grades **Found / Hunch / Unfound** (a negation pair, exempt from the edit-distance test, 4.10); check grades **Smooth / Jam / Botch**; DC tiers **Mild / Middling / Hard** (Smooth, Mild and Middling replace Clean, Low and Fair after the check: 5.3.24–5.3.26).

**Tier 2 (Later and M3+):** keywords enter with their content (Cord, Freefall, Clasp, Counterpiece, Peril tier). Each new keyword passes G3 against the whole glossary.

**Settings and system words given names here (MAIN pending items):** the visible difficulty setting below tier 0 is **Easier** (the setting reads "Difficulty: Easier"; the hub shows the **Easier lamp**); the telemetry consent row is **Share play data** (On/Off); the reserved run-modifier slot is the **Oath slot** on the Depth gauge (HL); the Later "Story" rung becomes the Easier rung and "Hard" keeps the CMP wording **Mortal companions**.

### 6.2 The Lore Codex (open; RS owns, CH 4.15.8 hosts; hook)
Pages unlock as the player plays: Residents (the Fireside), Old Maladies, Oddments, the Boss Ledger, Dream Rules pages, Hearsay. Flavour only; entries link to glossary keywords and never restate a rule differently.

### 6.3 Translator glossary
Every player-facing key carries: literal sense, register (4.2), tone note, grammatical gender and number, `article` field, plural form, translate / transliterate / keep, wordplay to keep or drop, and the narrowest widget it appears in. This is HL NR10 extended to every name (7).

### 6.4 Plain names (extends HL-27; agreed HL Q1 for the setting itself)
A display-only setting. When on, every flavour-layer name in a scope that has a Plain label is replaced on screen by a rule-word label. Data, ids, Whence and lore are unchanged.

| Scope | Plain label form | Example |
|---|---|---|
| Malady (HL-27) | `Malady: <rule tag>` | Choler → Malady: Easy Opening |
| Curse cost line (HL-27) | `Cursed: <rule tag>` | Plodding → Cursed: One Use Fewer *(illustr.)* |
| Oath (HL-27) | `Oath: <rule tag>` | Oath of the Short Fire → Oath: Shorter Rests *(illustr.)* |
| Charm (new) | `Charm: <output keyword> on <trigger>` | Hangnail → Charm: Helped on blocked harm |
| Trinket and notable property (new) | the property's keyword line | Strider → +1 speed *(illustr.)* |
| Enemy affix (new) | the granted keyword or a short rule | Plated → Heavy armour *(illustr.)* |
| Boss ability on the banner (new) | keyword effect under the boss name | Countdown → Area 3 · Dazed *(illustr.)* |
| Curiosities, themes, places, camp figures, given names | unchanged (whimsy is the content) | — |

Rules: every label is authored as one whole string in `plainLabel` (never built from a trigger fragment, 7.2); its tag, without the scope prefix ("Malady:", "Charm:"), is keyword-only and at most 24 characters (corrected after the check, with the charm example); **the rule tag alone is unique within its scope** (contests HL-27's prefix uniqueness; told plainly T7), so Ill-Omened's and the Spleen's shared "First Stumble" split (the curse cost line becomes "Stumbles Early"); the screen reader announces the Plain label **first** for scopes with obscure names (Maladies, curses, Oaths), even with the setting off (A11Y-3); labels pass G3, G8, G11 and G12.

---

## 7. Localisation and accessibility (lead's call; NP-2)

### 7.1 Length and pseudo-localisation
- Budget **+35%** for display names and **+50%** for strings under 10 characters (L10N-1). Every chip, plate, card and banner truncation test runs on pseudo-localised strings at that inflation before content lock. The caps in 4.2 are English source lengths checked against the narrowest widget at the inflated length.
- No ToUpper or ToLower on names; "VICTORY" becomes an authored "Victory" (ITEMS 8.2; NP-11). Turkish dotted and dotless i and German ß are why.

### 7.2 No assembled strings
- Every sentence is one whole-string key with named placeholders; translators get the full list of values each placeholder can take (L10N-3). Fragments that are assembled today are listed for the owners: "Oath of the <adj> <noun>" (HL; stored as whole names), "<Name> of ?" (IR; one key per family with the epithet slot), "<byname>. Felled the <boss>." (IR), "Maladies abroad: <names>" (HL-19; list template with locale separators), "Grace: <condition>" (EV), "Guards: <signature name>" (ENM 15.2).
- Names carry no article or possessive the grammar must change (L10N-4); the article field renders it.

### 7.3 Locale traps known now (NR10)
- **Choler**: Spanish *cólera* means cholera; `localeTermOverride` for es (and a check for Choler/Collared near-homophony in en speech).
- **Melancholia, Spleen**: everyday words in Romance and German locales; Melancholia keeps its mandatory tone sign-off and Atrabile fallback (agreed HL Q1).
- **Acedia**: Italian *accidia* is the deadly sin; locale review.
- **Hyphenated compounds and puns** (Grip-marks, Heart-won, Thumb-Worn, Road-Scuffed, Star-Crossed, Hound-Collar, Toll-Taker's, Log-Carrier, Slate-Sketcher, Roll-Reader): translator notes say what to keep (meaning, sound or shape).
- **Possessives with a trade noun** (Fowler's, Courtier's, Forager's, Scrollwright's, Lamp-trimmer's): translate the trade; note gender.
- **Proper nouns**: every name carries a translate / transliterate / keep flag. Recommended keep (transliterate if needed): Stairtop, Brask, Tolly, Old Ragged, Madame Ashes. Recommended translate: everything else.
- Each shipping locale runs its own blocklist, echo list, collision grep and native-speaker sensitivity read before M4 content lock (PROC-2; settled NR10 timing).

### 7.4 Accessibility
- **Spoken labels for every glyph** used in a name or verdict (A11Y-2): ⛨ "prevents", ⟲ "costs or reach", ✦ "channels", ◈ "stored moment", ▸ "button", ? "untold", ▲/▼ "stronger/weaker than usual", ⇄ "another way", = "same", ✕ "cannot use"; Rhythm strip beats "off beat / main beat / big beat" (BR 4.3.1; "Lead" and "Down" are a TTS homograph and an AN5 word).
- **Pronunciation or SSML hints** (A11Y-3) on Atrabile, Phrenitis, Pituita, Adustion, Jactitation, Tarantism, Acedia, Choler, Precentor, Pylorus, Antrum, Seneschal, Gilder, arras, valance, Wind-Down Hammer ("wind" as in clock). Maladies announce the Plain label first (6.4).
- **No TTS homograph** in a new name (lead, wind, tear, bow, wound, close, live, row): this rejected "Live Water", "Bow your head" and the "Lead beat" label.
- **No name is a UI button verb** (Rest, Answer, Kneel, Cancel, Back, Accept, Continue): this renamed Answer (heirloom line), Kneel, "Back to Stairhead" and kept Counterpiece payoff kinds internal.
- **No colour-only sibling pair** (A11Y-1): Oxblood Brigandine → Damask Brigandine; colour words stay only where icon and shape also differ (Grey Mantle).
- **No glyph-confusable spellings** (l/I/1, O/0, rn/m) in key names; none found in the finals.

#### 9.1.1 The string table (defined after the check)
- **Files.** One table per locale, `strings/<locale>.json`, owned by the lead; English (`en`) is the source. It is data, manifest-listed with the files above.
- **Keys.** `<doc>.<registry>.<id>` (for example `HR.ability.rekindle`, `TL.grade.open`): the document abbreviation, the registry and the record's internal id, which never changes (N-Q). Every record that reaches the screen binds to a key; a record with `internal: true` binds to none.
- **Fields per key:** `text` (the display string); `field` (`name`, `label`, `line` or `notice`: G9 and V24 r7 read `name` and `label` only; buttons are labels); `register` (4.2 row); `meaningId` (the glossary id, for a keyword or a deliberate reuse such as the boss menu's "Attack"); `properNoun`; `translate` (translate / transliterate / keep); `translatorNote` (literal sense, tone note and the wordplay to keep or drop, 6.3); `widget` (the narrowest widget, for the +35% check); `ssml` (optional); `plainLabel` (where 6.4 applies, authored whole); `waiver` (id, if any). Per locale: `article`, `gender`, and `plural` as CLDR categories (`one`, `few`, `many`, `other`, those the locale uses).
- **Greyed reasons and other raw ids** (`AlreadyWarded`, `spareForButton`, `TrinketHelpedLimit` and the rest): each document's owner lists every greyed-reason id of the document with a display string in the apply manifest (13), and the lead adds the keys **before M1 records**, so V24 r6 has nothing raw to find when it goes live at M1.

### 9.2 Content rules
- Every display name has a register row, an article value and a plural (V24 r8).
- Every waiver names its id, reason, approver and date (V24 r9); 9.3 now records all four.
- No record is written for a name still marked placeholder after its wave's freeze (10).

### 9.3 V24 `NamingPass` (registered by NP-1; owner the lead; runs on `V7.NameRegistry`; phase split per SV-20: static rows at build, string rows at content build)
| Rule | Check | Result |
|---|---|---|
| r1 | Exact equality across all registries (Classes 10, TK 15, AR 17.1 r13, V14 (f), V21 (g) pool), whole word, on display strings, fallbacks, Plain labels, epithets, bynames, given names and every `localeTermOverride`. **Two records that carry the same `meaningId` are one meaning, not a collision** (the boss menu's Attack and the universal action; Detonation and Rays and their templates; after the check) | fail |
| r2 | Retired name reused: a record whose (string, category, sense) matches a `retired_names.json` record; the same string in a sense listed there as live passes (after the check) | fail |
| r3 | Keyword collisions (G3): keyword stem inside a flavour name without the thing applying it; keyword pairs at edit distance ≤ 2 or sharing three first letters in a family (negation pairs exempt, 4.10); known homophone pairs; the AN5 stems Back and Down in place and action names | fail |
| r3b | Flavour near pairs (G4, added after the check): shared head word over the cap (4.9), edit distance ≤ 2, same first four letters and length band, filtered substrings (4.10) | flag |
| r4 | SRD (G5), IP floor (G6), sensitivity (G8; hanging forms and HL-register gendered terms fail, other gendered terms flag), gazetteer exact (G10) | fail (allowlist and waivers pass) |

| r7 | Premise words (G9) on `name` and `label` fields outside W-DREAM-1 and W-DREAM-HOLD-1, by the word forms of 4.7; "mimic" on screen | fail |
| r8 | Register row, caps at +35%, article and plural fields present; head-word cap (4.9); filtered substring rule (4.10) | flag (caps fail at content lock) |
| r9 | Waiver records complete (id, scope, reason, approver, date; expiry for a held waiver) | fail |

## 13. Changes to approved documents

Applied by the owners of the sections they touch, on the client's approval of this document; **this document edits no parent document itself**. Every row names its documents and sections; the renamed names for each row are the rows of 5 whose sites column names that document (5 is the exact list of names; each row below summarises).

**The apply manifest (lead's call, after the check).** Before any owner applies a row, the lead generates by script, and tags with each owner, one data file, `naming_apply_manifest.json`: one line per hit of every current name in 5 across the 34 documents, with file, line, current string, **sense tag** (the renamed sense, or the kept sense named in the row: Set the pike verb or Set the weapon set; Open the check grade or the door verb), **action** (change, keep, or internal), NP id and final string. Each NP row applies **every manifest line for its document** with action "change", and its section list is derived from the manifest, so the two cannot drift; the rows below and 13.1 are the readable summary. The manifest also carries each document's greyed-reason ids with their display strings (9.1.1). The **post-apply check** for every row is that every manifest line for its document is resolved (changed, or confirmed kept), not a bare grep, which cannot tell a kept sense of a common word from a renamed one; for a distinctive old name (Stairhead, Viaticum) a whole-word grep that finds it only in history notes, rename records and this document remains a sufficient second check. The **Wave** column says when: "Now" for the closing rows; "By wave (10)" for the rest, M1 names before M1 records and M2 names before M2 records. Rows that touch **decided**, **agreed** or **settled** text, or a client-confirmed name, are flagged ⚑ and name the answer that covers them: this document's questions, all answered with option A on 2026-11-24 (**agreed, Q1–Q6**; 0.2, 14.1), or a parent document's own answer. "Covered by agreed Qn" means the row applies that answer at the document's own sites; "(cites)" marks a row that only restates a name the question renamed. The approval column says who approves the row's text once this document is approved: the client's answer for the named question, the lead for mechanical rows. The documents and sections of every agreed rename with a distinctive string are listed by script in 13.1, and each row ends with its document's 13.1 cites, generated from 13.1 (after the check); agreed renames of common words (Set, Spare, boss Strike and Command, item dormant, the HP tier standard, the Commanded words Approach, Flee and Halt, Resistant (element), boons) are listed only in the manifest, where each hit carries its sense.

Hit lists include ordinary prose senses (surge, wary, silk, adept, artillery, minion, volley); only the sense named in 5 changes, and internal ids stay (N-Q). ## 14. Recorded answers and open items for the client

The six questions this document raised were answered by the client on 2026-11-24, each with the recommended option A **(agreed; final)**. The lead's answers (0.1), the style guide (4), the register (5) and every mechanical rename were accepted by the client by proceeding to the questions; they are recorded as the lead's answers and are confirmed with this document, **including every told-plainly item (T1–T15) and every new lead's call in 14.2**, and the lead's calls and told-plainly items made after the design check (14.4).

**Open for the client:** the approval of this document as a whole (status: draft, awaiting client approval), which covers the flagged rows of 13 (each names the agreed question or parent answer that covers it) and the new lead's calls made while finalising (14.2) and after the design check (14.4). **No question of this document is open.** One finding of the check needs the client's ruling because it touches two names the client chose: Windblown and Rampart are exact titles of other games (W-28; 14.4, "Needs the client"). Items that return only if triggered are in 14.3.

### 14.1 The six answers (agreed, 2026-11-24, final)

**Q1. Dream, sleep and wake words on screen (W-DREAM-1). Applied by NP-3, NP-16, NP-20, NP-21 (client rows) and, as cites, NP-4, NP-8, NP-10, NP-11, NP-17, NP-18, NP-22, NP-24, NP-25, NP-26, NP-28, NP-31, NP-32, NP-35, NP-36, NP-37 (13, 13.1).** **Option A (agreed).** The words are reserved (4.7). They appear only on (a) the run's UI frame (Dream card, track, step, Dream's Due, "the dream unravels", the Unwoken Roll and the rest of 4.7 (a)) and (b) named F5 Heart and Wake-theme content listed one by one (4.7 (b)), including "The Bed Turns Over" for the Night Governess hazard. The rest are renamed: Freefall, Peril tiers, Midwater, Alarm Order, Strangers, Clear Key (and, under 4.7 (c), Joins at 33%, Drowse, Oath of the Cold Iron and Heavy-Eyed). The Waking City stays gated until M4.
- *Rejected:* B, as A but also rename the Heart bed names ("The Mattress Heaves"; Foot of the Bed, the Turned-Down Bed and the two "Under the Bed" names); C, no reservation, keep every current dream name.

**Q2. Three sensitivity renames inside agreed or approved text. Applied by NP-5, NP-12, NP-20, NP-22, NP-28, NP-38 (client rows) and, as cites, NP-10, NP-11, NP-15, NP-17, NP-18, NP-19, NP-21, NP-23, NP-24, NP-25, NP-26, NP-27, NP-30, NP-31, NP-32, NP-36 (13, 13.1).** **Option A (agreed).** *Viaticum* (the Catholic sacrament given to the dying, in agreed LE Q1 text; RS 7.6 invented liturgies only; the "Lent" precedent) → **Parting Fare**. *Bedside Stand* (the Parting Fare host; puts a bed beside the keepers on F5, AN4) → **the Washstand**; the LP Q5 silhouette exception is unchanged. *Totem* (the Herald off-hand focus kind, approved in W, CL, HR; a living people's sacred word and a PHB subclass) → **Tipstaff** (heirloom "Thumb-Worn Tipstaff"; kit pick "Focus hand"), pending the M4 sensitivity read (fallbacks R8). Internal ids (`viaticumFallback`, `BedsideStandOnly`) stay.
- *Rejected:* B, Viaticum and Totem only, keep Bedside Stand; C, keep all three and defer to the M4 sensitivity read.

**Q3. The Hollow and Silk cluster. Applied by NP-20, NP-26 (client rows) and, as cites, NP-10, NP-16, NP-17, NP-18, NP-19, NP-21, NP-22, NP-23, NP-24, NP-27, NP-28, NP-29, NP-31, NP-35, NP-36 (13, 13.1).** **Option A (agreed).** The Silk Archive → **The Cobweb Archive** (short form "the Archive"; truthful to its Law of webs and silence); Hollowfolk → **the Fireside** (the V20 validator id `Hollowfolk` stays); "the Hollow" and Last Hollow stay, in the camp scope only ("the Hollow ahead" → "the next camp"); Wayward → **Windblown**. W-21 (Shade, one Team Cherry echo once the cluster is broken) stands.

**Q4. The difficulty ladder and MAIN's wording. MAIN 4.5's "feats, boons and upgrades" reads "offers", so "boon" means only the starting boon; "background perk" → **"Calling perk"**.

**Q6. Class names, the hub and the title. ### 14.2 Told plainly, and new lead's calls

Accepted by the client by proceeding to Q1–Q6 and confirmed with this document (the lead's answers, 0.1): every told-plainly item T1–T15, the new lead's calls of the synthesis, and the new lead's calls made while finalising against the client's answers (the last list below).

- **T1.** The literal reading of RS 7.6 and HL-30 NR2 ("no SRD names") would fail the corpus's own conditions, rarities, masteries and base families. 4.5 replaces it with a function test and an allowlist (NP-2).
- **T2.** The real-world gazetteer check (N5f) is new; RS 7.6 does not ban real places as written (the brief overstated it). It is a new lead's call.
- **T3.** The sensitivity blocklist outranks HL NR1: "obsolete for a hundred years" never clears a word that is now a slur.
- **T4.** Premise words are restricted in names only; flavour lines keep NR8 (the dream is the subject of Malady flavour) and AN4.
- **T5.** Unfiltered substring checks (V14 (f), NR6, V20s as written) are unworkable noise; 4.10 filters them.
- **T6.** CA V23s's "never print catch on any surface" is restated as "never a keyword or a name"; barks may say "catch". Approved text that prints it is renamed.
- **T7.** HL-27's Plain names were unique only by prefix; the tag must be unique.
- **T8.** The pass's own group drafts contradicted each other on about 40 names and several replacements broke the same rules; the critics' check fixed them (A.1).
- **T9.** 84% of harvested names stay as written (87% before the sense-aware recount after the check, 8.2); the pass is narrower than "rename every placeholder" suggests.
- **T10.** CH kept placeholders "the client confirmed". This pass renames seven Fireside figures (rule breaks: heal words, keyword collisions) and the Keeper's Cart (RC9 against "keepers of the sleep") as mechanical rows; only the umbrella Hollowfolk goes to the client (Q3).
- **T11.** Two places where the rule, not the name, should change are requests: the Treadwheel stove (HZ) and "Bring Them Home" (HR).
- **T12.** Peril tiers depend on the Perilous Door keeping its name; rename them together or not at all.
- **T13.** No trademark search was done; that is legal's step before a store page.
- **T14.** Weary keeps its word over Wary because the party condition is printed more often; the enemy state moves. Routed keeps its word over Rooted because it is agreed (C-3) and Rooted is Later.
- **T15.** CN 17's "scroll names follow the ability" yields to N13 when the ability name is an SRD spell.
- **New lead's calls (beyond the group drafts):** the W-DREAM-1 list structure (a)/(b)/(c); hanging words in the sensitivity list; the filtered substring rule; Tipstaff, Pale Mare, Tangled, Unbending, Deluge, Exhorting, Gall Slick, Sour Spit, Respite, Take Comfort, Bar the Way, Weatherwork, Arrowfall, Fresh Reserves, Load, Cast Out, Wand of Fetters, The Airing Cupboard, the Ironbiter, Lamp Water (each chosen after a critic rejected the drafts' candidates; A.1; Exhorting and Take Comfort are the after-check replacements of the synthesis's Banneret and Take Heart, 14.4); V24 and `NamingDrift`.
- **New lead's calls made while finalising (2026-11-24, after the client's answers; beyond the synthesis):**
  1. **"(agreed, Qn)" marks rows that only follow an answer.** Mechanical, keep and retired rows of 5 that apply an answer carry the same mark as its C rows: Q1 (Dreamfall, Lucid Key, Middle Dream, Other Dreamers, Wake Order, Wakes at 33%, Slumber, Oath of the Iron Sleep, Ill-Slept, The Waking City), Q2 (Totem kit), Q3 (Wayward, "the Hollow ahead", Last Hollow kept), Q4 (Nightmare Provisos), Q6 (Stairhead, "Back to Stairhead", Stairtop kept). The 58 C rows are unchanged in number (Q5 45, Q1 1, Q2 4, Q3 2, Q4 4, Q6 2).

XI 4.9.5's plate-mark cite follows (NP-25). Found while finalising: the synthesis's NP-14 and NP-25 did not list them.
