### 14.4 After the design check (2026-11-24): lead's calls, told plainly, and what needs the client

A design check read this document after the client's answers, with four lenses (consistency, clarity, tone, builder). Each finding was fixed, merged with another, or rejected with a reason. **No client answer is overridden:** where a finding would change a name the client chose, the name stands, its cost is told plainly below, and only W-28 goes back to the client.

**Lead's calls, after the check** (confirmed with this document):
1. **Appendix B re-run sense-aware** (8.2): per " / " part and per parenthetical sense, against the row of 5 for that sense and source document; 127 outcomes corrected; W-DREAM-1 entries read "(a)" or "(b)"; waiver outcomes name their id; the CA names CA itself renamed or rejected read "retired". Counts recomputed: 2,428 final, 329 renamed in whole or part, 83 under a waiver, 43 internal, 7 retired or deleted, 3 held (0, 8.2).
2. **The apply manifest** (13): one sense-tagged line per hit, from which each NP row's sections derive; the post-apply check is "every manifest line resolved"; NP-4 to NP-38 apply by wave (M1 names before M1 records, M2 before M2; 10).
3. **NP rows and 13.1**: each NP row ends with its document's 13.1 cites, by script; 13.1 adds the remaining distinctive Q1, Q3 and Q5 renames (the Q5 role words, Bull Rush, the Q5 (c) notables among them); 0.2's Change cells and 14.1's "Applied by" lists are regenerated from the rows; ⚑ flags name the parent answers whose wording a mechanical rename touches (CH Q4 and Q5, decided MAIN 4.7, LP Q4, EP Q1, VG Q1 and Q3, BR Q3), with F8 and A.1 corrected; NP-29 adds Lingerer and the "Seeded" menu row; NP-7 closes CMP-27 with no AR change, since AR 12.3 already reads Levy soldier.
4. **Retirement by sense** (4.11, 9.1): `retired_names.json` keys (string, category, sense) and lists kept senses; The Waking City is **held** under the time-boxed W-DREAM-HOLD-1 (expires at the M4 content lock), not retired, so the client's waiver path stays open (agreed Q1).
5. **W-DREAM-1 made exact** (4.7): (a) adds Dreambook pages and "dreamed option(s)"; (b) adds, one by one, The Wake for You, The Bedpost Ladder, bed clearing, pillow rooms, the "dust pillow" Keg skin and the dust-pillow seam; (c) reads "on any name not listed in (b)"; G9 matches explicit word forms as whole words or compound parts (bedroll(s) excepted); r7 reads name and label fields only. Renamed: Dream Fragments → Fragments, "bed hanging" → "canopy valance", "crib bars over glass" → "rail bars over glass"; the "Heart sleeping-draught nurse" example is deleted. The EP catalogue titles H1 Sleepwalkers and H6 Bedpost Watch stay internal (4.8).
6. **Hanging words** (4.6): explicit forms (hang … gibbet) as whole words or compound parts; Hangnail kept under W-25; "drawn curtain" → "drawn arras"; the lock state "hanging" → "dangling".
7. **Gendered terms** (4.6): fail on the HL registers (NR2's own scope), flag elsewhere; W-26 keeps the period role titles and the agreed Marksman.
8. **The Gut theme says "gall"** (4.6): bile square → gall square, The Bile Low → The Gall Low.
9. **Nineteen mechanical finals replaced**, each with its reason in 5: Patch Up (was Dig In: it named bracing, not a heal), Take Comfort (Take Heart met Hearth), Smooth (Clean met the Clear Key), emptied field (cleared met Clear), Mild and Middling (Low was the HP mark; Fair sat in Fairground and sounded like Fare), Foul Play (Bank Shot promised a ricochet), Upheaval (Groundswell held Swell), Vicelike and Vicelike Gauntlets (a third Grip), Antidote kept (Venomproof read as resistance; Antitoxin is gone), Exhorting (Banneret was a noun), Spear Through (Running Thrust held "run"; Driving and Skewer are weapon verbs), Tuck Tight (Heavy Blanket brought back the airway), The Groaning Board (Banquet Run read as a mode), drawn arras (a hanging word; Pall is a Herald art), Toll-Taker's Shield (Pass-Watch held Watchful's stem), failsafe ("trigger" had three meanings), Returning hire (Held-over held Held). New row: Bodyguard (affix) → Challenger (5.4.47).
10. **Templated numbers** (4.3): ladder tiers print "Peril {n}" in Arabic numerals, as the client's answer wrote it; upgrade ranks print Roman numerals (Slot Renewal II); neither breaks the digit rule.
11. **Register and gate rules** (4.2, 4.9, 4.10, 4.5): buttons and labels up to 4 words (18 / 20 characters); a mirror affix takes the conditions cap (12); "head word" defined; G3 counts Back and Down in place and action names; negation pairs (Found, Unfound) exempt from the edit-distance test; N5e and N5f fail a dictionary word only where it names a store-facing or coined thing.
12. **V24 and its data** (9): r1 by meaning id, r2 by sense, r3b for flavour near pairs, r6 by key, r7 on name and label fields; `waivers.json` (every waiver with approver and date) and `glossary.json` (83 core keywords, with the Commanded words) added; the string table defined (9.1.1), with per-locale article, gender and CLDR plurals; every greyed-reason id gets a display key before M1 records.

**Told plainly after the check** (the client should hear these; none reopens an answer):
- **TP1. "Run" (agreed Q5) also names the unit of play.** It is kept as decided and prints only after "Commanded:" with the keyword icon (W-27; 4.8). If playtest shows players reading it as the run, the check's alternative was "Begone" (0 hits); that change would need the client, because the word is the client's.
- **TP2. "The Downstair" (agreed Q6) brings back "down",** the AN5 word this pass removed elsewhere (Lay Down Arms, "Go down again"). Kept as decided (W-27); the recorded fallback is still the Exit Stair (R6).
- **TP3. Marksman (agreed Q5) is a gendered word.** Kept under W-26; an ungendered word ("Sniper", 1 prose hit) exists if the client ever wants one.
- **TP4. Some agreed Q5 names guess imperfectly:** Fracturing may read as breaking the target (it breaks your own weapon); Crouch reads as a stance rather than Prone; Arrowfall says arrows on a property that crossbows and slings also carry; "Direct" beside "Attack" can read as an adjective; "Fast Grip" can read as quick. Each is kept as decided; the rule line and icon carry the truth (N4a's opaque-guess clause), and the Grip cap is held by renaming Clench instead.
- **TP5. Watchful (agreed Q5) shares six letters with the Herald art Watchfire,** and the Clear Key (agreed Q1) sits one letter from the first check-grade final "Clean": the mechanical names moved (Smooth, Toll-Taker's Shield), the agreed ones stay (W-27).
- **TP6. The first outcome count was wrong.** The matcher ignored senses, so 127 entries read "final as written" that 5 renames or rules internal. 84% of names stand, not 87%.
- **TP7. Several names this document called final broke its own rules** (hanging, bed, crib, bile, age imagery) or reversed CA's own renames (the Underpillow and the CA knots). They are fixed in 5, 4.7 and Appendix B.

**Needs the client (one item):**
- **W-28. Windblown (agreed Q3) and Rampart (agreed Q6) are exact titles of other games,** found by the echo check after the answers. Rampart (Atari Games, 1990) fails N5e as a subclass name, the same ground on which Bastion was renamed; Windblown (Motion Twin, 2024, a roguelite like this game) is an ordinary adjective on an item part and is flagged, not failed. Options: **(a) recommended:** rename Rampart through the Name Gate (the lead's candidate is "Redoubt", 0 hits, subject to G1–G14 and the store search) and keep Windblown under W-28 as a recorded flag; (b) rename both; (c) keep both under W-28. Until the client rules, both stay as agreed under W-28, which expires before the first record that prints either name.

## 15. Risks

| # | Risk | Signal | Response |
|---|---|---|---|
| R1 | Restatements leave old names behind (the Upended pattern) | `NamingDrift` > 0; unresolved apply-manifest lines | V24 r2 fails retired senses at build; every NP row closes only when its manifest lines are resolved (13) |
| R2 | Renames touch far more than the first estimate of about 90 sections: 13.1 alone lists 851 document-sections for the agreed renames, and the mechanical rows add more (corrected after the check) | Owners queue; manifest lines unresolved at a wave's gate | Rows are display strings only; the apply manifest splits the work by document and wave (M1 names first, then M2; 10); ids stay |
| R3 | A new final collides with a later document | V24 r1–r3 | The registry runs on every new segment (SV-20) |
| R4 | The allowlist becomes a loophole | SRD hits pass as "adopted" | Each allowlist entry names its attribution and source section; additions are lead's calls |
| R5 | Plain names and keyword discipline make the game read dry | Playtest | Tone lives in voice (barks, Whence, Reveries); the whimsy budget keeps meta names free |
| R6 | Downstair still reads as the hub ("Stair" stem) | Playtest the "To the Downstair" action | Fallback: Exit Stair (0 hits at check; A.1) |
| R7 | Peril tied to the Perilous Door | A later rename of the Door | Rename both together (T12) |
| R8 | Tipstaff fails the sensitivity read or reads as legal jargon | Sensitivity read | Fallback candidates Sceptre (0 hits) or Bough (0); not Rattle or Effigy |
| R9 | W-DREAM-1 creeps (new Heart names assume the waiver) | New (b) entries | (b) is a list, not a scope: each new entry is a lead's call recorded in 9.1 |
| R10 | Locale traps beyond those known (7.3) | Native read | PROC-2 per locale before M4 lock |
| R11 | The title or a marketed name is not clearable | Legal search | SEARCH-1 before any store page; this pass picks no title |
| R12 | Echo clusters re-form through new content | V24 r5 cluster test | The echo list is data and grows |
| R13 | Prose hits on common finals (Found, Mild, Smooth, Attack, Run) hide future collisions; Run also names the unit of play | Grep noise; a player reads "Run" on a plate as the run | These are keywords in `glossary.json`; V24 checks them by meaning id, not by prose grep; Run prints only after "Commanded:" (W-27) |
| R14 | Darkest Dungeon (Heirloom + Deeds) and Hades (boon) echoes kept | Player or press comparison | Recorded waiver W-11; revisit if a second echo of either studio appears (cluster test) |

- The string-table fields of 9.1.1 for every key (`field`, `register`, `meaningId`, `properNoun`, `translate`, `translatorNote`, `widget`, `ssml`, `plainLabel`, `waiver`, and per locale `article`, `gender` and CLDR `plural`), and `internal` on records that never print: text and data, not code; ids never change (N-Q).
- Seeding the ten data files of 9.1 from their sources: `srd_names.json` from the SRD 5.1 and 5.2 documents themselves, never from memory (LEGAL-2); the echo list, gazetteer and per-locale sensitivity parts; each allowlist entry with its attribution line (R4).
- Translator notes, SSML hints and +35% pseudo-localisation fit checks for every key (7.1, 7.4), and whole-string keys wherever a sentence would otherwise be assembled (7.2).
- Rules Glossary tooltip and entry wording inside the closed keyword set (6.1), and Lore Codex entries (6.2; RS owns, CH hosts).
- Generating the apply manifest by script and tagging each line's sense with its owner (13); applying each NP row's manifest lines with the document owner, wave by wave (10); the post-apply check that every manifest line is resolved (12).
- Names for content not yet written (Later classes and conditions, the Death-Ward-shaped spell, LE's Blood Price, the CH hooks, new (b) entries): proposed by builders, run through the Name Gate G1–G14 (4.10), each a lead's call recorded in the registry; any rename of an approved-final name comes back to the client (4.11).
- **Not tuning:** the W-DREAM-1 list and the reserved premise words **(agreed, Q1)** (a new (b) entry is a lead's call recorded in 9.1, never a scope widening, R9); Parting Fare, the Washstand and Tipstaff **(agreed, Q2)** (Tipstaff changes only through the M4 sensitivity read's fallbacks, R8); the Cobweb Archive, the Fireside, Windblown (subject to the client's W-28 ruling) and the camp-only Hollow **(agreed, Q3)**; Peril tiers with the Perilous Door, renamed together or not at all, "offers" and "Calling perk" **(agreed, Q4)**; the three Q5 bundles **(agreed, Q5)**; Vanguard, Shade, Arcanist, Herald, the Stairtop, the Downstair, Ascetic and Rampart (Rampart subject to the client's W-28 ruling) **(agreed, Q6)**; the N-rules of 4; no runtime string assembly (7.2); internal ids unchanged (N-Q).

### A.2 Names checked (case-insensitive, whole word, `docs/superpowers` specs and review; re-run while writing, 2026-11-24)

- **Every final in 5** is checked; the count is in 5's last column. Finals with 0 hits are free. Finals with hits were read: keywords and keyword printing rows (Bleeding, Corroded, Chilled, Demoralised, Found, Unfound, Attack, Run, Come, Wait, Stored, Used, Treasure field, Worn items, Sprawled, Levy soldier) are the same meaning by design; prose-only hits (Knock 34, Sustain 26, Load 127, Idle 76, Receive 9, Trapped 29, Planted 9, Defender 12, Guardian 7, Direct 8, Swell 5 (Organ Swells, same theme), Din 2, Clasp 4 (a cut trinket and prose), Retaliating 4 (the condition it mirrors), Reflection 2, Long Note 1 (the CH line it echoes), the Dais 1, the Newel 1 (CH's own candidate), Jawbone 1 (CA's object column), Watchful 3 (cut item names), "the next camp" 19 (same sense), Detonation and Rays (the templates themselves), Brask 6 and Turnkey/Inmates (registered roster names)). **Read after the check:** Forecourt 201 (85 as "the Forecourt": the same room, already its on-screen name in RS and FL; the earlier "0" was wrong), Fragments 39 (RS and EV already use the short form, same sense), Challenger 7 (CON's "the challenger", same sense), Smooth 5 and Mild 5 (prose), dangling 3 (prose).
- **Candidates rejected because of hits found in this check:** Mired (an existing condition), Unbowed (an existing armour property), Outpour (an RT rule), Dismissal (the CMP companion action), Emblem ("Fire Emblem" in prose and leader-emblem charms), Regulars (XI density label Regular), Shelter (AR property), Snap (Snap Casting), Green Room (BR reserve socket), Stairwell (CH prose), Rattle (25 hits), Linen Press (CH nursery prop).
- **Sites of every current name** (5, sixth column) are whole-word counts per document by script; a current name with 0 sites is one the drafts introduced and this pass rejected (Running Noose).
- **Names the source documents had not checked, now checked:** SH Old Wound, Bad Footing, Blind Side, Trigger Shot (pass); HR Opening Cry (renamed), Waver, Pick the Question, Strike or Shove (pass), Rush the Colours (pass), Bring Them Home (request, NP-38); BR Probate Room, Clapper Swing, Inkblot, Flex and Stamp, Mouthful, Cell Zero Slam (pass), See Also (prose hits in FL and EP; kept as a Weaver move; the boss's Cross-Reference became Footnote).
- **Prefix and validator ids:** `\bNP-[0-9]+` 0 hits; `\bV24\b` 0 hits.
- **Echo and gazetteer check of the finals (after the check).** The 337 finals were read against a curated list of well-known game, film and book titles (the lead's list; the binding check is V24 r5 and r4 on `echo_list.json` and `gazetteer.json` at M2). Exact titles found: **Rampart** (Atari Games, 1990; a subclass, so N5e fails it) and **Windblown** (Motion Twin, 2024, a roguelite; an ordinary adjective on an item part, so flagged): both client-agreed, held under W-28 for the client (14.4). Dictionary words that are also titles and pass under N5e's refinement, flagged only: Tangled (a 2010 film; a condition), Undying (an affix), Onslaught (a pool), Reflection (an enemy).
